example. Counts are not proof strength.}
\label{nh:wpo:tab:verify}
\end{table}

The sampled profiles use a small concrete representation of full and partial
fibres and check the inclusion criterion, not the rank of a truncation standing
in for an infinite rank. The absorption tests use bounded coefficients; the proof
of Lemma~\ref{nh:wpo:lem:absorb}, not their range, establishes the identity. The
recorded runtime, about $9.2$ seconds, is not a performance guarantee. No finite
test computes an uncountable order type from samples. The checks do not
establish Wolk's theorem, the input component heights, the transfinite upper
bound or novelty, and ``two programs can share an incorrect mathematical
premise'' (09): the chain construction and the deferred-absorption proof are the
justification for the infinite theorem.

\subsection{Foundational scope and a formalization route (09)}\label{nh:wpo:sec:foundations}

All inputs of an instance are sets; the fibre family, the set of component
heights and the set of linear extensions are finite. The proof works for
uncountable fibres and exponents without large-cardinal hypotheses; it is a
theorem scheme over set-sized ordinals, not a claim about a set of all ordinals
or a proper-class linear extension. For ordinal fibres the lower-bound chains are
explicit initial-segment chains. For WPO fibres the finite schedule is computable
from the weights, but selecting height-attaining chains in the components uses
the classical maximal-chain theorem: a nonuniform input, not an algorithm, so the
computability phenomena of \cite{MMS2012} are not bypassed.

A formal development can separate the finite optimization from the transfinite
mathematics: first the support-profile theorem, then the finite natural-sum bound
and deferred absorption, then the main upper proof as a finite induction over the
support values of a chain, and the lower proof as an ordinal-indexed
concatenation of component chains with checked cross-block inclusions. The
classical input should be stated exactly as ``a WPO has a chain attaining its
rank-defined height'', never as a replacement of rank by a supremum of chain types
for arbitrary well-founded orders. A separate finite layer should prove
\eqref{nh:wpo:eq:dp}, certificate soundness and relabelling invariance; a checker
verified there would certify values conditional on the transfinite theorem and the
component-height lemmas. No Lean file is included or claimed to have been
compiled; a future formalization must report which classical inputs are proved in
its library and which, if any, are admitted.

The boundaries are concrete. The skeleton is finite, since the number of support
changes and the sums of bounded terms must be finite. The component powerset
heights are positive pure powers, which supplies additive indecomposability,
limits and the absence of internally completed fibres. Components are WPOs, which
supplies closure and chain attainment. The order is the standard
Hoare/finitely-generated-downset order; a different powerset ordering need not
satisfy the profile rule. The empty skeleton needs its own case (the formula's
empty maximum would be $0$, while $\K(\varnothing)$ has height $1$), and allowing
the finite weight $1$ without correction is false, since a singleton has a
two-element finitary powerset. 09's proof audit lists the same boundaries: empty
$Q$ is separate; finite or successor components are not admitted to the
pure-height theorem; general nonpure WPO components are not covered by the
ordinal CNF expansion; infinite skeletons need new limit-stage arguments; the
theorem computes height, not width or maximal linear-extension type; it does not
justify repeated application of $\K$ without new component-height calculations;
and set-sized instances in ZFC are used, with no proper-class rank object.

09's dependency audit, in brief: the profile normal form needs the finite
skeleton and no finite cofinal subset in a fibre (a completed fibre need not
be compact internally); the product chain bound needs strictly increasing
coordinate ranks, finite natural addition and persistent coordinates bounded
by actual later elements; deferred absorption needs finite Cantor normal forms
and a strictly smaller bounded term; the main upper bound combines profile
comparisons, the finite support partition, the product bound, absorption,
refinement and Wolk's theorem; the lower bound needs nonempty height-attaining
component chains, varying a coordinate only just before it disappears; the
compressed formula needs the canonical antichains and exact equality of
retirement sets at one-vertex steps; the limit-fibre theorem is a finite order
isomorphism by literal block expansion, finite and successor terminal blocks
excluded; exponent chambers need only the finite exponent preorder; and the
certificates check every state and edge, with no transfinite theorem
machine-checked.
\clearpage
\phantomsection\part*{Closing the addition}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Closing the addition}

\section{Research questions of the addition}\label{nh:sec:questions}

The four sources propose nine (07), twelve (09), eight (11) and nine (12) further
questions. None of them is asserted to be a previously recognized or numbered open
problem in the literature, except for the broader published extension programme
already cited; 09 adds that its directions are ``not all asserted to be previously
unstudied'' and that each should first be compared with existing results. Where
one source's question is answered by another, it is re-scoped here rather than
printed as open.

\subsection{Questions answered or partly answered inside the addition}

\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Finite tails and successor fibres} (07 Question~1; 09 \S14.1, with the
milestone ``fibres of the form $\omega^\rho c+k$''): \textbf{answered} for
ordinal-chain fibres by Theorem~\ref{nh:thm:arbitrary} (12) and the exact model
\eqref{nh:eq:mixed} (11). The ``extra rank data attached to a frontier'' that 07
asked for are the all-antichain states with fallback weight $1$; the empty-downset
and greatest-element corrections that 09 anticipated are exactly the states that
maximal frontiers of the expanded skeleton would discard
(Section~\ref{nh:sec:examples}).
\item \emph{Rank-level refinement and a direct point-rank formula} (07
Question~9, second half; 09 \S14.3): \textbf{answered} for ordinal-chain fibres by
Theorems~\ref{nh:thm:localrank} (12) and~\ref{nh:thm:local} (11), the latter for
any finite persistent system. 09's hope that a point-rank formula could remove the
appeal to chain attainment in the ordinal-fibre upper bound is met differently:
the proofs of Part~II never use chain attainment. For WPO fibres the point-rank
question stays open.
\item \emph{Non-chain fibres} (07 Question~2; 12 Problem~10.6; 11 Problem~12.1):
\textbf{partly answered} by Part~III, for WPO fibres whose finitary powersets have
positive pure height. 12's Proposition~\ref{nh:prop:generalcoordinates} gives an
abstract non-chain version without a support representation. Still open: nonpure
component heights (09 \S14.2: does equality of $\hgt(\K(P_q))$ still determine the
output when those heights are not pure; if not, find a small counterexample and a
replacement invariant describing bounded initial segments; Theorem~\ref{nh:wpo:thm:limits}
does not answer this, since it expands actual ordinal fibres), and 11's question
whether a finite family of principal-ideal height profiles could replace the
ordinal-coordinate phases, with finite products of ordinal chains carrying their
local rank functions as a first target. 07 asked whether a pure value of
$\hgt(\K(P_q))$ together with ``suitable uniform upper-cone rank information''
suffices: Part~III shows the pure value alone suffices for WPO fibres, at the price
of Wolk's theorem. 12 asked whether pure height, a chosen least element and
cofinal-chain data suffice on a nontrivial class; any positive answer must prove
the representation conditions separately, since equal scalar heights do not
identify which coordinates survive.
\item \emph{Complexity} (07 Question~4; 09 \S14.4; 12 Problem~10.3):
\textbf{partly addressed} by Corollary~\ref{nh:cor:NP}, NP membership of the
threshold problem for the explicit pure-block encoding. A polynomial-time algorithm
or an NP-hardness result, even for two positive scales, would sharpen it; the short
output of Corollary~\ref{nh:cor:smalloutput} does not make the path problem easy.
07 asked in particular about deciding whether the leading or a specified lower
coefficient exceeds a threshold when $Q$ is given only by its order relation and
exponent pattern, before enumerating frontiers; 09 asks whether the uniform case
(largest number of nonempty retirement groups) and the mixed case (a
lexicographically significant coefficient vector) are known ordered-set or
set-system optimization problems, and to separate input-comparison cost from
combinatorial cost before any hardness claim. Any hardness claim needs a separate
reduction, not supplied here.
[Added 29 September 2026, batch 42: the two preceding sentences describe the
state before Part~IV, which \textbf{answers} this item for the hardness
alternative and supplies the reduction. The threshold problem is NP-complete
for height-two skeletons with every fibre $\omega$, one scale
(Theorem~\ref{nh:ur:thm:np}), hence for the pure-block encoding of
Corollary~\ref{nh:cor:NP} (Remark~\ref{nh:ur:rem:partII}); 09's uniform case
is a uniquely restricted matching number, its mixed case an ordered-matching
objective, and 07's leading-coefficient threshold is NP-complete at one scale
(Section~\ref{nh:ur:sec:questions}).]
\item \emph{The report's own next target} (Section~\ref{nh:sec:remainedopen}):
\textbf{answered}, by Theorems~\ref{nh:thm:frontier}, \ref{nh:thm:arbitrary}
and~\ref{nh:wpo:thm:main}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Questions that remain open}

\begin{problem}[Coefficient-compressed algorithms; 12 Problem~10.1, 07 Question~3, 09 \S14.6]
\label{nh:prob:compression}
Compute the height directly on Cantor-normal-form coefficients given in binary,
without expanding a coefficient $n$ into $n$ blocks, and determine the complexity
in the coefficient bit lengths and the size of the original skeleton.
\end{problem}
Block expansion proves computability but can blow up before ideal enumeration even
begins. A first target is a fixed skeleton with arbitrary coefficients and a fixed
number of exponent scales (12); even an efficient rule for two interacting
repeated chains would help, and it must keep lower-order terms that survive only
for particular retirement interleavings (09). The issue is succinct finite
combinatorics, not ordinal notation; chains and antichains give closed-form test
cases (07). A compressed algorithm must account for finite tails rather than
discard them (11).

\begin{problem}[Structural and fixed-parameter algorithms; 12 Problem~10.2, 09 \S14.5, 11 Problem~12.5]
\label{nh:prob:fpt}
Beyond Corollary~\ref{nh:cor:chaincover} and the $n^{O(b)}$ bound of
Section~\ref{nh:wpo:sec:algorithms}, find algorithms of the form $f(k)N^{O(1)}$ for
a parameter $k$: the width of $Q$ or of $\M(Q)$, the number of distinct exponent
priorities or capacity levels, a decomposition width of the comparability or cover
graph (bounded treewidth, series--parallel skeletons), or the number of distinct
upper-neighbour sets, avoiding the full antichain or phase expansion.
\end{problem}
The relevant parameter may be the number of simultaneously persistent coordinates
rather than the number of frontiers; a proof must identify which partial-state
information permits safe merging of histories under noncommutative ordinal addition
(12). The present solvers are polynomial in an explicit state graph that may be
exponentially larger than the input (11).
[Added 29 September 2026, batch 42: for height-two skeletons Part~IV gives
algorithms of the form $2^aN^{O(1)}$ in the number $a$ of distinct
upper-neighbour sets, and FPT algorithms in the number of distinct upper
neighbourhoods and in the vertex-cover number
(Corollaries~\ref{nh:ur:cor:rowtypes}, \ref{nh:ur:cor:types}
and~\ref{nh:ur:cor:vc}); the number of distinct exponent priorities admits no
FPT or XP algorithm unless $\mathsf P=\mathsf{NP}$
(Corollary~\ref{nh:ur:cor:priorityhard}). The problem stays open for the other
parameters and for taller skeletons.]

\begin{problem}[Ordinal width and maximal linear-extension type; 12 Problem~10.4, 07 Question~5, 09 \S14.8, 11 Problem~12.2]
\label{nh:prob:width}
Compute the ordinal width (and, 09, the maximal linear-extension type) of the same
mixed-fibre finitary powersets, beginning (11) with persistent systems with two
distinct positive capacity levels and state posets of bounded width. In
particular (07), can the finite weighted frontier data
\eqref{nh:eq:frontierweights} that compute the height also compute the ordinal
width, or must they be enriched by information about concurrent rather than
sequential coordinate changes?
\end{problem}
The height proofs concern decreasing sequences; antichain sequences need not have
chain-ordered labels, so the finite chain cover is unavailable in that form and a
chain certificate no longer controls an antichain rank tree (07, 11, 12).
Vialard's treatment of widths of Cartesian products of ordinals
\cite{VialardWidth2025} is a starting point (11). Theorem~\ref{nh:wpo:thm:ambiguity}
already shows that coarse local data lose information; for these invariants the
replacement for bounded persistent progress may involve antichain or bad-sequence
residuals (09). Part~I's own width question (Section~\ref{nh:sec:remainedopen})
is unchanged.

\begin{problem}[Repeated finitary powersets; 12 Problem~10.5, 07 Question~6, 11 Problem~12.3]
\label{nh:prob:iteration}
Find a finite representation, containing finite ordinal lexicographic sums and
closed under $\K$, on which $\hgt(\K^r(S))$ is computable for every finite $r$, or
at least $\hgt(\K(\K(S)))$ on a substantial subclass without reduction to finitely
many numerical samples.
\end{problem}
The first output generally has product-like pieces rather than chain fibres, so it
is not another finite lexicographic sum of chains; this is the exact obstruction to
an iteration theorem from Theorem~\ref{nh:thm:arbitrary} (11, 12). The output height
is not a sufficient description of the first powerset, and even stratum isomorphism
types are insufficient without their attachments (Corollary~\ref{nh:cor:strata});
a symbolic presentation of profiles and transitions (07), or of finite antichains of
persistent tuples (11, whose state component need not remain finite), is a
plausible start. Proposition~\ref{nh:prop:generalcoordinates} suggests building
blocks but not the required support representation.

\begin{problem}[Infinite skeletons and infinite state orders; 12 Problem~10.7, 07 Question~7, 09 \S14.7, 11 Problem~12.7]
\label{nh:prob:infinite}
Extend the theorems to well-founded or well-quasi-ordered infinite skeletons, or to
infinite persistent-state systems, under explicit compactness, bounded-frontier or
controlled-support-evolution hypotheses.
\end{problem}
Finiteness is used for the maximum of finitely many tree ranks, for keeping finitely
many smaller monomials below a pure power, for the existence of a largest active
capacity, and for the finite depth correction in \eqref{nh:eq:peelR}; infinite unions
of well-founded trees can acquire infinite branches, finitely bounded progress can
accumulate at a limit stage, and a support need not be captured by a finite chain of
states. Replacing a maximum by a supremum is not a proof and may not be the right
statement (07, 09, 11, 12); increasing ordinal skeletons are a test class (09).

\begin{problem}[Minimal presentations, chambers and schedules; 12 Problem~10.8, 07 Question~8, 09 \S\S14.10--14.11, 11 Problems~12.4 and~12.6]
\label{nh:prob:presentations}
Characterize which weighted finite frontier graphs arise from finite skeletons
with ordinal fibres (07). Characterize when two finite persistence presentations,
or two weighted frontier graphs arising from finite skeletons, give the same
height for every order-preserving exponent substitution, and find minimal or
canonical presentations; find a stronger equivalence that also preserves every
point rank. Classify, for a fixed skeleton, all output coefficient vectors as the
exponent preorder varies, and represent them compactly without enumerating all
ordered partitions of the labels; determine which skeletons admit one schedule
optimal in every chamber (09). Describe equivalence classes of linear extensions
with the same retirement groups or value.
\end{problem}
Exponent invariance removes the numerical names of scales; a further reduction must
preserve lifetime intersections and retirement opportunities, and equality of active
maxima or of stratum heights is too weak (11, 12). Edges that never lie on a
maximizing path may be redundant, and deciding universal redundancy is a finite
symbolic optimization (07). The compact frontier model shows that height-only
compression can exist without a pointwise-rank identification, so the two goals
should be studied separately (11). Two parallel chains show that a schedule optimal
in every chamber need not exist, so the question is which skeletons admit one; an
intrinsic characterization could say more than enumerating all weak-order
patterns (09). Zero-weight insertions and absorbed lower terms
create redundancy that a quotient of schedules could remove, giving smaller
certificates (09).

\begin{problem}[Formal verification and constructive scope; 12 Problem~10.9, 07 Question~9, 09 \S14.12, 11 Problem~12.8]
\label{nh:prob:formal}
Formalize the persistence theorem (or the retirement-box theorem and the
normalization, or the compressed-frontier theorem and the support certificate
checker) together with a certificate checker, and identify the weakest set-theoretic
or type-theoretic setting in which the rank maps, attaining chains and checker can be
developed uniformly for effective notations below a fixed bound.
\end{problem}
The arguments use finite choices and well-founded ordinal recursion, not a
large-cardinal principle, and the $\eps$ bound of the code is a notation boundary,
not an expected theorem boundary (12). For WPO components one should identify the
minimum component-witness interface that turns a finite schedule into a represented
infinite chain, keeping classical assumptions and algorithmic content visible (09).
No reverse-mathematical equivalence is claimed, and for applications to termination
a separate norm and control function would be needed: ordinal height alone does not
bound all finite execution lengths (11).

\begin{problem}[Noetherian spaces and Hoare powerspaces; 09 \S14.9]
\label{nh:prob:noetherian}
Which parts of the support-profile argument survive for a finite gluing of
Noetherian spaces under a lower-Vietoris or Hoare construction?
\end{problem}
The combinatorial normal form and the chain-attainment step should be audited
separately: a topological rank need not share every chain property of a WPO, so the
present proof does not establish a topological generalization. The neighbouring
report on Hoare powerspace statures treats a different, topological question.

\begin{problem}[Editorial: relation between Part~III and Proposition~\ref{nh:prop:generalcoordinates}]
\label{nh:prob:editorial}
Can Theorem~\ref{nh:wpo:thm:main} be derived from
Proposition~\ref{nh:prop:generalcoordinates}, applied with coordinates
$\K(P_q)$ (least element $\varnothing$), Wolk's theorem and the canonical frontier of
Lemma~\ref{lem:canonical}? The lower bound would need 09's base-point chains, since
$\K(P_q)\setminus\{\varnothing\}$ has no least element. This question was raised
during the merge and has not been checked; none of the four sources observes it.
\end{problem}

\section{What the addition does not claim}\label{nh:sec:nonclaims}

Every limitation stated by any of the four sources is kept. Parts~II and~III compute
height and individual ranks; they give no formula for ordinal width or for maximal
order type or maximal linear-extension type of the powersets, no theorem for
iterated or repeated finitary powersets, no theorem for infinite skeletons or
infinite state systems, and no large-cardinal consistency or inconsistency
conclusion. They do not solve the unrestricted finitary-powerset problem for
arbitrary well-partial-orders (07) or settle the published programme (12). Part~III
covers WPO fibres only under pure positive powerset height; general nonpure WPO
components and finite or successor WPO components are not covered, and replacing
$\hgt(\K(P_q))$ by $\hgt(P_q)$, a maximal linear-extension type or a width is not
justified. Complexity statements count ordinal operations on explicit state graphs;
none is a polynomial-time bound in the skeleton size or in a binary encoding of
Cantor coefficients, and NP membership is not NP-completeness.
[Added 29 September 2026, batch 42: Part~IV proves NP-completeness
(Theorem~\ref{nh:ur:thm:np} and Remark~\ref{nh:ur:rem:partII}); the rest of
the preceding sentence stands.] There is no bound on
lengths of controlled executions, and no claim that the first rank maps of
Theorem~\ref{nh:thm:persistence} are pointwise exact. The executable notations stop
below $\eps$ (07's code takes positive integer exponents and is ``not a general
parser for arbitrary ordinal notations''; 09's symbolic labels are unchecked), with
guards of $20$ (12), $22$ (09) and $18$ blocks or $4{,}096$ refined states (11).

No elementary supporting lemma is claimed with priority (07), and the product-height
arithmetic, Wolk's theorem and the uniform formula are credited to their sources.
Each source's literature search was targeted and uneven, and non-discovery is not
evidence of novelty. Earlier preprint versions of Vialard's lexicographic-product
paper have different authorship and carry an author-disagreement notice; they must
not be conflated with the published record \cite{Vialard2025} (07), and no theorem is
borrowed from them (11). Each source's audit is a self-audit, not a referee review
(12). The sources state that they made no repository write, and none of them
assumes the correctness of the unrefereed theorems of Part~I; their
repository comparisons are report-relative, and no review of every repository
theorem, or build of the repository, is asserted (09, 11, 12). The computations
certify finite identities and implementations only; they are not proof-assistant
checks, not computations of infinite descending-tree ranks, and not certificates of
novelty.

\section{Provenance of the addition and choices made in merging}\label{nh:sec:choices}

The addition was written from four manuscripts (Table~\ref{nh:tab:sources}), all
pinned to \texttt{e21766d04}, batch-36 manuscripts 07, 09, 11 and 12, arrived in
\texttt{e13affd32} and placed in \texttt{1a1396d4d}. Their contributions:
12 is the base of Part~II (persistence theorem, ideal-state program, arbitrary
fibres, point ranks, NP membership, chain-partition bound, scale invariance,
non-chain coordinates, product benchmark); 11 supplies the peeling proof and upper
certificate, the pair-based program, the exact all-antichain isomorphism, phase
refinement as a theorem, point ranks in any persistent system, and the successor and
local-rank examples; 07 supplies the normalization, the chain-cover proposition, the
retirement-box theorem and its second proof of the frontier formula, saturation, the
paired stratum-types example, the diamond, universal bounds, the product lemma for
limit factors and the frontier-graph program; 09 is Part~III.

The merge had to choose in the following places.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Base and parts.} 12 is the base for chain fibres because it is the most
general there (all ordinal fibres, point ranks) and uses no chain-attainment theorem.
09's theorem is incomparable with 12's and keeps its own part and hypotheses.
\item \emph{Duplicates.} A result proved by several sources is printed once with its
provers named. Genuinely different proofs are kept as marked routes: 11's peeling
proof of Theorem~\ref{nh:thm:persistence}, 07's normalization-and-box proof of
Theorem~\ref{nh:thm:frontier}, 11's direct proof of \eqref{nh:eq:mixed}, 11's route to
point ranks, and 07's proof of the product lemma for limit factors. The tree-union
lemma, block realization, the no-return inequality, uniform recovery, exponent
invariance and the coefficient bounds were merged across sources.
\item \emph{Conventions.} Empty exits weigh $1$ in Part~II and $0$ in Part~III, with
the difference displayed in Remark~\ref{nh:wpo:rem:conventions}. The maximization
domain is stated for all nonempty chains, with the saturated and maximal restrictions
proved equivalent. Symbols follow Part~I, and every renamed symbol is listed in
Table~\ref{nh:tab:notation}; 09's $\alpha_q$ became $h_q$ to keep heights and fibres
apart. 09's examples were relabelled to this report's $N$-poset; the persistent
example uses Part~I's $x$, $y<z$ throughout.
\item \emph{Hidden hypotheses checked when splicing.} 07's normalization uses
$1+x<\alpha_q$ and so needs infinite pure fibres, which Section~\ref{nh:sec:frontier}
assumes; 11's peeling assumes $\rho_q>0$, handled by convention~3; 09's lower bound
needs Wolk chains; 12's non-chain proposition needs least elements.
\item \emph{Editorial additions.} Remark~\ref{nh:rem:threenames},
Problem~\ref{nh:prob:editorial} and the example after
Corollary~\ref{nh:cor:scale} showing that its positivity hypothesis is needed
are the merge's own and are marked as such. The
placement-time cross-check of the four programs is reported from the placement commit;
its script is not shipped.
\item \emph{Bibliography.} Entries were merged into Part~I's list. 11's entry for
Vialard's lexicographic-product paper, which gave no DOI, is replaced by Part~I's
published record \cite{Vialard2025}. The Hilton, Schmidt, Wolk, Marcone--Montalb\'an--Shore
and Vialard width entries are new.
\item \emph{Part~I.} Its labelled text is unchanged, and so are its section,
theorem and equation numbers with one exception: equations are numbered
continuously and Part~I's appendices now follow Parts~II--III, so the
truncation formula of Appendix~\ref{sec:lex}, equation (24) in the pinned
article, is now \eqref{eq:truncation}. Sentences in Part~I that said
no nonuniform height formula was known or claimed were kept as provenance of the
pinned state and given dated pointers to this addition. The delivered data files
\path{data/weighted_N_result.json} and
\path{data/mixed_height_counterexample_result.json} still say ``No nonuniform
height formula is asserted.''; they predate the addition and stay byte-identical.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Conclusion of the addition}\label{nh:sec:conclusion}

The missing quantity in a mixed-fibre height calculation is not the height of a
stratum but the length of a coordinate that can be exhausted before leaving it (12).
Charging a coordinate only when it retires gives an exact formula because two
mechanisms match: a natural-sum rank bound that keeps the still-active coordinates,
and a concatenated chain that exhausts one disappearing coordinate at each stage;
a finite cover of the descending-sequence tree then makes the global maximum exact.
Three further routes reach the same formula: a leading-exponent peeling induction
(11), a normalization onto maximal supports with a box theorem (07), and, for WPO
fibres, deferred absorption of bounded persistent progress together with Wolk's
theorem (09). The result is a complete height and point-rank calculus for finitely
generated downsets of finite lexicographic sums of ordinal chains, including finite
and successor fibres, an exact extension to WPO fibres of pure powerset height, and a
precise explanation of Part~I's persistent counterexample. Width, iteration,
non-pure general fibres and infinite skeletons remain separate research tasks. The
manuscripts are unrefereed; independent checking and a fuller priority search remain
necessary before the addition is treated as a refereed or historically certified
contribution.

\phantomsection\part*{Part IV.\quad Uniquely restricted matchings: the height-two problem is NP-complete}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Part IV.\texorpdfstring{\quad}{ } Uniquely restricted matchings: the height-two problem is NP-complete}

\noindent\emph{This part is batch-42 manuscript 04, added on 29 September
2026. It answers the complexity item of Section~\ref{nh:sec:questions} and
the remark closing Section~\ref{nh:wpo:sec:examples}, and partly answers
Problem~\ref{nh:prob:fpt}. Its uniform theorem is proved without any theorem
of Parts~I--III; its nonuniform transfinite readings rest on
Theorem~\ref{nh:wpo:thm:heighttwo}. Equations of this part are numbered
within sections, so that no equation number of Parts~I--III or of Part~I's
appendices moves.}

\section{Provenance, conventions and the answer}\label{nh:ur:sec:intro}
\setcounter{equation}{0}

\subsection{The question}\label{nh:ur:sec:question}

Theorem~\ref{nh:wpo:thm:heighttwo} (Part~III) expresses the height for a
height-two skeleton as an optimization over first upper neighbours, and the
text after it said that the computational complexity of this finite
set-system scheduling problem was ``not classified here''. The complexity
item of Section~\ref{nh:sec:questions} asked whether the uniform and
nonuniform problems correspond to established ordered-set or set-system
optimization problems, and asked for a polynomial-time algorithm or an
NP-hardness result, even for two positive scales.
Problem~\ref{nh:prob:fpt} asks for fixed-parameter algorithms in structural
parameters, including the number of distinct upper-neighbour sets.

The answer of this part (manuscript 04) is that the uniform optimization is
exactly a classical matching invariant. Thus the hoped-for unrestricted
polynomial algorithm would imply $\mathsf P=\mathsf{NP}$. The obstruction
does not require complicated ordinal notation, many scales, large
coefficients, or a tall skeleton: it already occurs with a $0$--$1$
incidence matrix and fibres all equal to $\omega$. At the same time, the
weighted problem has additional structure not captured by one
maximum-cardinality matching. Its lower ordinal terms can distinguish
maximum matchings that are indistinguishable at the leading scale.

\begin{resultbox}
\small\textbf{Main results (04).} For a finite nonempty skeleton $Q$ of
height at most two, with bipartite incidence graph $G_Q$, and a uniform
fibre $\alpha=\omega^\rho$, $\rho>0$,
\[
 \hgt\bigl(\K(S_\alpha(Q))\bigr)=\alpha\cdot\bigl(\nuur(G_Q)+1\bigr)
 \qquad\text{(Theorem~\ref{nh:ur:thm:uniform})},
\]
where $\nuur$ is the maximum uniquely restricted matching number. The proof
is a direct ranking and an attaining chain. With the published
NP-completeness of bipartite uniquely restricted matching \cite{GHL2001},
deciding $\hgt(\K(S_\omega(Q)))\ge\omega\cdot(k+1)$ is NP-complete
(Theorem~\ref{nh:ur:thm:np}). For nonuniform pure priorities the finite
objective of Theorem~\ref{nh:wpo:thm:heighttwo} is an ordered-matching
maximum (Theorem~\ref{nh:ur:thm:weighted}) and obeys an exact
priority-layer recurrence (Theorem~\ref{nh:ur:thm:layers}).
\end{resultbox}

\subsection{Contribution and dependency ledger}\label{nh:ur:sec:ledger}

The logical boundaries of this part are as important as the formulas.
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}p{0.29\linewidth}X@{}}
\toprule
Result & What is proved or imported\\
\midrule
Uniform height formula & A direct ranking and an explicit chain are proved here;
no appeal to the report's general height theorems.\\
Matching characterization & The classical alternating-cycle characterization is
reproved. Its application to retirement schedules is made explicit.\\
NP-completeness & The reduction to ordinal height is proved here. Bipartite
uniquely restricted matching NP-completeness is the published input of
Golumbic--Hirst--Lewenstein.\\
Weighted ordered matchings and priority layers & Finite combinatorial theorems
proved here, with no transfinite assumption.\\
Nonuniform height interpretation & Uses Theorem~\ref{nh:wpo:thm:heighttwo},
restated precisely in Remark~\ref{nh:ur:rem:import}.\\
Algorithms and certificates & Exact finite correctness proofs plus executed
Python tests; no proof-assistant certification.\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}

These distinctions prevent two misleading conclusions. First, identifying a
repository question with a known NP-complete problem is not a new proof of the
historical NP-completeness theorem itself. Second, tests of finite schedules do
not verify transfinite ranks. The uniform rank proof below supplies its own
mathematical justification; the more general weighted application has the
separate inherited dependency just stated.

\subsection{Literature and novelty positioning}\label{nh:ur:sec:literature}

Golumbic, Hirst and Lewenstein introduced and studied uniquely restricted
matchings, including their bipartite NP-completeness and their relation to
matrix-rank bounds~\cite{GHL2001}. Legal set-covering sequences and the Grundy
covering number are also established notions; Bre\v{s}ar, Henning and Rall
explain their connection with dominating sequences~\cite{BHR2016}. The
unweighted set-system parameter below is therefore not proposed as a new
invariant.

The contribution of 04 is a proof-level connection to this report's ordinal
height problem, a direct uniform transfinite proof, the ordered rather than
unordered weighted formulation, and the residual priority calculus. These are
continuations of the inspected report. A targeted literature search does
not establish that every formulation is unprecedented. The claim is a
resolution of the identified repository question with fully stated
dependencies, ``not a claim of a previously unrecognized historical
breakthrough'' (04), and ``not an unspecified famous open problem in matching
theory'' (04's proof audit).
