The antichain input has no lower or upper vertices in the finite objective and
an explicit terminal parameter. It represents a nonempty antichain of pure
fibres through their maximum height, not the empty skeleton.

\section{Verification, reproducibility and limits}\label{nh:ur:sec:verification}
\setcounter{equation}{0}

\subsection{Executed checks}
The recorded run used Python~3.13.5 with pseudorandom seed $20260929$. It
performed the following exhaustive families. For every $0\le r,s\le3$ it
enumerated every $r\times s$ binary incidence matrix with no zero lower row,
every assignment of lower priorities from $\{1,2,3\}$, and each terminal
priority in $\{1,2,3\}$. This yields $31{,}755$ weighted instances. Zero
columns are permitted. For each one the subset program was compared against
all permutations, the covered-row program, all admissibly ordered matchings,
and the priority-layer recurrence, and its certificate and twin compression
were checked.

The run also enumerated all $15^4=50{,}625$ nonzero-row $4\times4$ incidence
matrices with every priority equal to $1$, comparing the program against all
upper permutations. This is an exhaustive incidence-matrix test, not an
enumeration of unlabelled graphs and not a check of every weighted $4\times4$
case. An additional $120$ seeded weighted instances used up to five vertices
per side, with lower priorities selected from $1,2,5,9,10^{12}$ and terminal
priorities from $1,3,8$. The large-priority tests check that no expansion
proportional to the numerical exponent occurs.

\begin{table}[htbp]
\centering\small
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}Xr@{}}
\toprule
Recorded assertion category & Count\\
\midrule
Subset program versus all upper permutations, weighted suite & 31,884\\
Subset program versus covered-row program & 31,884\\
Subset program versus all admissibly ordered matchings & 31,884\\
Subset program versus the priority-layer recurrence & 31,884\\
Full Bellman certificate verification & 31,884\\
Twin-compression invariance & 31,884\\
Maximum high endpoints cover the high rows & 33,245\\
Closure-system height versus matching number & 445\\
Uniform $4\times4$ program versus all permutations & 50,625\\
Tampered terminal-value rejection & 9\\
Malformed mathematical input rejection & 5\\
\midrule
Total recorded assertions & \textbf{275,633}\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\caption{04's recorded assertion counts, from
\texttt{data/\allowbreak 06-ur-matchings-verification.json}.}
\label{nh:ur:tab:assertions}
\end{table}
The counts $31{,}884$ include the $31{,}755$ exhaustive weighted instances,
the $120$ seeded instances and the nine named examples. The exact ledger is
\path{data/06-ur-matchings-verification.json}; the console record
\path{data/06-ur-matchings-verification_console.txt} prints two count lines
followed by the same ledger. Not every category is run on every family.

\subsection{Independence and what was not checked}
The brute schedule evaluator locates each row's first neighbour by a position
map, rather than by the production program's incremental group rule. Its
ordinal normalizer scans the monomial word from right to left, rather than
using the production left-to-right ordinal adder. The ordered-matching
evaluator checks all forbidden earlier cross-edges directly. The priority
recurrence independently enumerates maximum high matchings and residuals.
These comparisons reduce implementation-specific error risk; they are not a
substitute for the proofs.

Some components remain shared: the instance representation and the basic
matching enumeration are reused by validation paths, and the certificate
checker shares ordinal arithmetic with the optimizer. The counts are numbers
of assertions, not measures of proof strength. No finite test establishes a
transfinite theorem, exhausts arbitrary graphs, verifies an arbitrary ordinal
notation system, or establishes historical novelty. No Lean or Rocq source was
compiled as part of this work. The finite graph results hold for arbitrary
allowed priorities; the tests do not establish that extension.

\subsection{Formalization roadmap (04)}
A small formal development can be organized without importing the report's
entire transfinite theory. First formalize finite bipartite incidence,
triangular matchings and the productive-witness equivalence. Next formalize
ordinal words as finite priority--coefficient lists, prove their
normalization semantics, and verify the Bellman checker. Then prove the
priority recurrence over the finite set of occurring priorities, using the
coverage lemma and the ordered-matching principle as separate dependencies.

For the transfinite endpoint, formalize the support-profile description and
the strict map \eqref{nh:ur:eq:rankmap}; the attaining-chain construction then
proves the uniform theorem directly. This separates the graph-theoretic layer,
the finite ordinal-algorithm layer and the set-sized rank layer. The
nonuniform height application should be enabled only after
Theorem~\ref{nh:wpo:thm:heighttwo} is itself proved or explicitly assumed. A
future formalization should report its axioms and assumptions rather than
labelling Python execution as kernel checking.

\subsection{A compact proof-dependency map (04)}
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}p{0.30\linewidth}X@{}}
\toprule
Endpoint & Essential dependencies\\
\midrule
Productive count $=\nuur$ & Fresh witnesses; triangular order;
alternating-cycle characterization, reproved.\\
Uniform transfinite height & Finite progress function
$\operatorname{prog}(X)$; exact downset profiles; natural-sum rank map;
explicit $\alpha$-blocks.\\
Uniform NP-completeness & Uniform height theorem; published bipartite
uniquely restricted matching NP-completeness; explicit polynomial reduction.\\
Weighted matching formula & Fresh maximum-priority witnesses; weak
monotonicity of ordinary addition.\\
Priority-layer recurrence & Weighted formula; high-subgraph matching bound;
coverage lemma; residual low-row characterization.\\
Weighted height conclusions & Above finite theorems plus
Theorem~\ref{nh:wpo:thm:heighttwo}.\\
Bellman certificate soundness & Finite subset induction; all candidate
inequalities; one attaining predecessor.\\
Structural FPT bounds & Equal-neighbourhood compression; subset program;
elementary vertex-cover type count.\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}
The part contains conventional mathematical proofs for these arrows, not
formal proof terms. It is suitable for external review and for the staged
formalization described above.

\section{Research questions of Part~IV}\label{nh:ur:sec:questions}
\setcounter{equation}{0}

\subsection{Questions of Parts~II--III answered or partly answered here}

\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Complexity} (07 Question~4; 09 \S14.4; 12 Problem~10.3; the item
of Section~\ref{nh:sec:questions} and the closing sentence of
Section~\ref{nh:wpo:sec:examples}): \textbf{answered} for the NP-hardness
alternative. The threshold problem is NP-complete already for height-two
skeletons with every fibre $\omega$, one exponent scale
(Theorem~\ref{nh:ur:thm:np}); with Corollary~\ref{nh:cor:NP} the threshold
problem for the explicit pure-block encoding is NP-complete
(Remark~\ref{nh:ur:rem:partII}). 09's uniform case (largest number of nonempty
retirement groups) is the maximum uniquely restricted matching number and the
Grundy covering number of the neighbourhood family
(Theorem~\ref{nh:ur:thm:productive}, Section~\ref{nh:ur:sec:grundy}); 09's mixed
case is the ordered-matching objective of Theorem~\ref{nh:ur:thm:weighted},
which 04 does not identify with a previously named problem. 07's
leading-coefficient threshold is NP-complete at one scale
(Corollary~\ref{nh:ur:cor:priorityhard}), and the leading coefficient is a
uniquely restricted matching number (Corollary~\ref{nh:ur:cor:leading}).
Input-comparison cost is separated from combinatorial cost as 09 asked:
exponents are binary labels copied, never expanded
(Proposition~\ref{nh:ur:prop:weightednp}). The ``separate reduction'' that
item called for is Theorem~\ref{nh:ur:thm:np}, from the published
NP-completeness of bipartite uniquely restricted matching \cite{GHL2001}.
Nothing here gives a polynomial algorithm, which would imply
$\mathsf P=\mathsf{NP}$.
\item \emph{Structural parameters} (Problem~\ref{nh:prob:fpt}):
\textbf{partly answered} for height-two skeletons. The number of distinct
upper-neighbour sets of lower rows gives a $2^aN^{O(1)}$ algorithm
(Corollary~\ref{nh:ur:cor:rowtypes}); the number of distinct neighbourhoods
of upper vertices and the vertex-cover number are also FPT parameters
(Corollaries~\ref{nh:ur:cor:types} and~\ref{nh:ur:cor:vc}). The number of
distinct exponent priorities is \emph{not} an FPT or even an XP parameter
unless $\mathsf P=\mathsf{NP}$ (Corollary~\ref{nh:ur:cor:priorityhard}).
Width parameters, decomposition widths and all skeletons of height three or
more remain open.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Further questions (04)}

The complexity question and the height-two neighbourhood-type parameter have
been addressed above. The following are further targets relative to these
results; 04 does not assert that all of them are previously unstudied in the
literature.

\begin{problem}[Weighted forests; 04]\label{nh:ur:prob:forests}
Determine the complexity of the ordered ordinal objective on incidence
forests. Can the priority-layer recurrence be implemented in polynomial time
without enumerating all maximum high matchings? A successful tree dynamic
program must remember enough boundary information to distinguish the two
residual outcomes in Example~\ref{nh:ur:ex:tie}.
\end{problem}
The unweighted forest problem is elementary because every matching is
uniquely restricted. That fact does not resolve the weighted precedence and
residual selection problem. A first milestone is a complete algorithm for
paths with arbitrary binary priority labels and an explicit proof that local
state merging preserves all surviving lower coefficients.

\begin{problem}[Weighted decomposition-width algorithms; 04]\label{nh:ur:prob:width}
Find exact algorithms parameterized by an incidence-graph decomposition width,
with priorities part of the input. Seek substantially better dependence than
the double-exponential vertex-cover bound of Corollary~\ref{nh:ur:cor:vc}.
Specify whether the algorithm handles the whole ordinal or only its leading
coefficient.
\end{problem}
An algorithm for maximum-cardinality uniquely restricted matching alone is
insufficient: one needs either ordered boundary data or a compositional form of
the residual priority theorem. The number of priorities by itself is excluded
as a tractable parameter by Corollary~\ref{nh:ur:cor:priorityhard}. This is
the height-two case of the decomposition-width clause of
Problem~\ref{nh:prob:fpt}.

\begin{problem}[Compression of maximum high endpoint sets; 04]\label{nh:ur:prob:endpoints}
For fixed $\Qhi$, characterize when two sets in $\mathscr S_{\mathrm{hi}}$
produce equivalent residual objectives. Can one enumerate only a small
representative family, perhaps parameterized by the number of low
neighbourhood types or by a separator?
\end{problem}
The recurrence proves that the internal ordering of a chosen high matching is
irrelevant after that layer, but its upper endpoint set may be indispensable.
A useful certificate would describe equivalence of residual objectives without
solving each residual instance independently.

\begin{problem}[Succinct Cantor coefficients; 04]\label{nh:ur:prob:succinct}
Extend the algorithms to ordinal-chain capacities specified by binary Cantor
coefficients, without replacing a coefficient $c$ by $c$ separate pure blocks.
Handle finite tails and successor corrections explicitly.
\end{problem}
This is Problem~\ref{nh:prob:compression}, which stays open. The present
binary priority representation does not answer it: storing the exponent
$10^{12}$ as a label is different from processing a fibre
$\omega^\rho\cdot10^{12}$ without expansion. A fixed skeleton with two
repeated interacting chains is an appropriate first test, as 12 also
suggested.

\begin{problem}[Beyond height two; 04]\label{nh:ur:prob:beyond}
Identify a finite combinatorial object playing the role of a triangular
matching for general finite skeletons. It must record vertices that can be
both newly activated and later retired, not merely a single lower-to-upper
incidence.
\end{problem}
The height-two reduction depends on every nonmaximal vertex retiring at a
first maximal neighbour. Intermediate levels create multiple lifetimes and
precedence constraints. Any proposed generalization should reduce to the
present matching formula and preserve the retirement-path formulas already
known in this report (Theorems~\ref{nh:thm:frontier}
and~\ref{nh:wpo:thm:main}).

\begin{problem}[Nonpure non-chain components; 04]\label{nh:ur:prob:nonpure}
For lexicographic sums of well-partial-orders whose finitary powersets have
nonpure heights, find the local rank information needed in place of a single
pure monomial per fibre.
\end{problem}
The current finite calculus assumes one pure contribution at each retirement.
The report's ordinal-chain block expansion does not automatically transfer to
arbitrary non-chain components. A small counterexample to scalar-height
substitution, or a provably sufficient finite profile invariant, would be a
substantive next step rather than another application of the pure formula.
This is the nonpure clause of the non-chain item of
Section~\ref{nh:sec:questions} (09 \S14.2), which stays open.

\begin{problem}[Short upper certificates; 04]\label{nh:ur:prob:certificates}
Determine useful graph classes on which a matching lower witness and a
low-rank matrix with the same zero--nonzero pattern always certify the
optimum. More generally, find polynomial-size upper certificates for
structured priority-layer residuals that avoid a full $2^s$ Bellman table.
\end{problem}
The field can matter, as Example~\ref{nh:ur:ex:cycle} shows. Matrix-rank
bounds are sound but not asserted to be complete. Any general certificate
claim should be reconciled with the NP-completeness result, rather than
inferred from successful small examples.

\begin{problem}[Counting and stability; 04]\label{nh:ur:prob:counting}
Count optimal upper schedules or optimal high endpoint sets in tractable
incidence classes, and classify how these sets change when two adjacent
priority levels exchange order.
\end{problem}
Strictly increasing relabelling preserves all maximizers, but changing the
weak-order pattern can erase or restore lower terms. A chamber-by-chamber
analysis could expose combinatorial structure hidden by the single optimum
coefficient vector. Compare the chamber questions of
Problem~\ref{nh:prob:presentations} (09).

\begin{problem}[Certified integration; 04]\label{nh:ur:prob:formal}
Formalize the finite matching and Bellman layers, then connect the checker to
the direct uniform ordinal rank theorem. Extend the trust boundary to the
nonuniform case only after its transfinite scheduling theorem is checked.
\end{problem}
This is a concrete route to a machine-checked answer to the original
complexity question, and a height-two instance of
Problem~\ref{nh:prob:formal}. It avoids making the finite computation depend
on an unnecessarily large library while preserving a precise theorem-level
connection to the actual finitely generated downsets.

\section{What Part~IV does not claim, and choices made in writing it}
\label{nh:ur:sec:nonclaims}
\setcounter{equation}{0}

\subsection{Non-claims}
Every limitation stated by 04, in its article, delivery README and proof
audit, is kept. The manuscript is AI-assisted and unrefereed; it is not a Lean
or Rocq formalization and no proof-assistant source was compiled. No
historical priority is claimed; the unweighted matching and covering-sequence
notions are classical and credited, and the targeted literature search does
not establish that every formulation is unprecedented. The NP-hardness is
imported from Golumbic--Hirst--Lewenstein; 04 does not reproduce or claim a new
proof of the original matching hardness reduction. The nonuniform transfinite
readings import Theorem~\ref{nh:wpo:thm:heighttwo}, which 04 neither re-proves
nor formally checks; only the uniform height theorem, and the finite weighted
theorems, are proved independently. There is no polynomial-time general
solver, no approximation ratio for an ordinal objective, and no proof that
matrix-rank bounds are always tight. Arbitrary higher-skeleton scheduling,
nonpure WPO substitution and binary Cantor-coefficient compression are not
solved. The solver is a pure-positive-priority solver, not an implementation
for finite or successor fibres; its guard of $22$ upper vertices is not a
performance guarantee, and the certificate checker certifies the finite
optimum, not the transfinite interpretation. The finite tests are
implementation checks, not proofs of transfinite statements and not evidence
of historical novelty. 04 did not exhaustively compare the report archives
that had newly arrived at its pin, and states that it resolves an identified
repository question, not an unspecified famous open problem in matching
theory.

\subsection{Choices made in writing Part~IV}
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Placement.} 04 continues Part~III's height-two theorem and
Section~\ref{nh:sec:questions}, so it is added to this report as a fourth
part, after the closing part of Parts~II--III and before Part~I's appendices.
\item \emph{Numbering.} Equations of Part~IV are numbered within sections, and
the equation counter is restored after this part, so that no section,
theorem or equation number of Parts~I--III or of the appendices moves;
in particular \eqref{eq:truncation}, which already moved once in batch~36,
keeps its number.
\item \emph{Notation.} 04's symbols were renamed as in
Table~\ref{nh:ur:tab:notation}: its $L$, $U$, $Z$, $N(\ell)$ follow Part~III's
renaming of the same sets; $S$, $\tau$, $H$, $K_H$, $d$, $R$, $F$, $D(C)$, $V$,
$p$ and $\operatorname{cl}$ were renamed because each already names something
else in this report. No normalization changed: every sum is ordinary unless
written $\ns$, and empty maxima are $0$, as in Part~III.
\item \emph{Repository input.} 04's ``Theorem (repository input)'' is printed
as Remark~\ref{nh:ur:rem:import}, a restatement of
Theorem~\ref{nh:wpo:thm:heighttwo}, not as a new theorem; 04's citations of
``Theorem~35.1'', ``Section~37'' and the pinned article became
cross-references.
\item \emph{Editorial additions.} Remarks~\ref{nh:ur:rem:secondroute}
and~\ref{nh:ur:rem:partII}, the identification of 04's examples with this
report's $N$-poset and with 09's height-two example, the relations to
Part~III's support-ideal program, and the placement-time check of
Section~\ref{nh:ur:sec:crosscheck} are the merge's own.
\item \emph{Stale sentences.} Sentences of Parts~II--III that said the
complexity was not classified, that a hardness claim needed a separate
reduction, or that NP membership is not NP-completeness, were kept as
provenance of the pinned state and given dated pointers to this part. The
abstract on the title page gained one dated sentence, and the title page's
top spacing was reduced by $12$\,mm so that it still fits on one page.
\item \emph{Bibliography.} 04's entries for Golumbic--Hirst--Lewenstein,
Bre\v{s}ar--Henning--Rall and Francis--Jacob--Jana were added to the report's
list; its entry for this report itself became internal cross-references.
\item \emph{Files.} 04's code, data, examples, proof audit and source record
are shipped byte-identical under the prefix \texttt{06-ur-matchings-}; its
examples, delivered in \texttt{examples/}, are in \texttt{data/}. The shipped
text still uses the delivered names; the README maps them and gives a rerun
recipe that works on a copy.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Conclusion of Part~IV}\label{nh:ur:sec:conclusion}
\setcounter{equation}{0}

For height-two skeletons, uniform finitary-powerset height is governed by a
classical graph invariant:
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \text{height coefficient}-1
   &=\text{maximum productive schedule length}\\
   &=\text{maximum uniquely restricted matching size}.
 \end{aligned}
\]
The equivalence supplies an NP-completeness proof with only the fibre
$\omega$, and the explicit ordinal ranking establishes that conclusion
independently of the inherited weighted height theorem. It also identifies
the finite maximal frontier height and gives immediate exact formulas for
forests and cycles.

For unequal priorities, the correct object is an admissibly ordered matching.
The leading coefficient comes from the highest-priority incidence subgraph;
all lower coefficients are optimized in residual graphs selected by maximum
high matchings. This separates what is determined by matching cardinality
from what depends on preserving low-priority rows. Safe twin compression,
structural parameter bounds and finite Bellman certificates turn the theory
into an exact computational continuation, while leaving the genuinely new
transfinite and succinct-encoding questions visible.

\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Appendices of Part I}
\appendix
\section{Finite nonuniform lexicographic sums}\label{sec:lex}

This appendix gives a useful consequence of the same absorption theorem.
It concerns the lexicographic sum itself, not its finitary powerset.

For a nonzero ordinal
\[
 \eta=\omega^{\delta_1}a_1+\cdots+\omega^{\delta_s}a_s,
 \qquad \delta_1>\cdots>\delta_s,
\]
define $\deg(\eta)=\delta_1$ and
\[
 \tr_{\ge\tau}(\eta)
     =\sum_{\{j:\delta_j\ge\tau\}}\omega^{\delta_j}a_j.
\]
The latter is the initial Cantor-normal-form part whose exponents are at
least $\tau$.

\begin{lemma}[Replacing a fiber by a maximal linearization]
\label{lem:replacement}
For a finite poset $Q$ and WPOs $P_q$, let $\eta_q=\ot(P_q)$. Then
\[
 \ot\left(\lxs_{q\in Q}P_q\right)
   =\ot\left(\lxs_{q\in Q}\eta_q\right).
\]
Empty fibers can be deleted from the index poset, retaining its induced
order on the remaining indices.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Choose maximal linearizations of the fibers. Replacing their orders by
these linearizations adds comparisons to the lexicographic sum, so the
right-hand side is at most the left-hand side.

Conversely, take an arbitrary linearization $L$ of the original sum.
Its restriction to $P_q$ has some type $\zeta_q\le\eta_q$. After identifying
each restricted fiber with its ordinal type, $L$ is a linearization of
$\sum_q^{\mathrm{lex}}\zeta_q$. This sum embeds as an induced subposet in
$\sum_q^{\mathrm{lex}}\eta_q$, by initial-segment embeddings within each
fiber. Consequently
\[
 \operatorname{type}(L)
 \le\ot\left(\lxs_q\zeta_q\right)
 \le\ot\left(\lxs_q\eta_q\right).
\]
Taking the maximum over $L$ proves the reverse inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Successor-degree truncation]\label{thm:truncation}
Let $Q$ be finite and let $P_q$ be WPOs with $\eta_q=\ot(P_q)$. Ignore
indices whose fibers are empty. For every remaining $q$, put
\[
 \tau(q)=\max\bigl(\{0\}\cup\{\deg(\eta_r):r>q,\ \eta_r>0\}\bigr).
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:truncation}
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 \ot\left(\lxs_{q\in Q}P_q\right)
    =\bigns_{\{q:\eta_q>0\}}\tr_{\ge\tau(q)}(\eta_q).}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:replacement}, it suffices to use the ordinal fibers
$\eta_q$. Expand each of their finite Cantor normal forms into a finite
sequence of pure blocks, repeating a block according to its coefficient.
Within a fiber these blocks appear in nonincreasing exponent order.
Between fibers, order every block of $q$ below every block of $r$ whenever
$q<r$. This produces a finite pure-block stratification with full
comparisons between comparable blocks, hence in particular a finite
cofinal stratification.

A block of exponent $\delta$ is not absorbed by a later block of its own
fiber, since internal exponents never increase. It is absorbed by a block
of a strict successor fiber exactly when some such fiber has leading
exponent greater than $\delta$. Therefore it survives exactly when
$\delta\ge\tau(q)$. Theorem~\ref{thm:strata} now says to take the natural
sum of all surviving blocks, which is \eqref{eq:truncation}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Recovery of the finite uniform formula}

Suppose all $n$ fibers have the same nonzero type
$\eta=\omega^\delta c+\sigma$, where $\sigma<\omega^\delta$, and let $k$
be the number of maximal elements of $Q$. Every nonmaximal fiber loses
its tail $\sigma$, while every maximal fiber retains it. Thus
\[
 \ot\left(\lxs_{q\in Q}P\right)
    =\omega^\delta\cdot(cn)+(\sigma\np k).
\]
This is the finite-index specialization of the uniform lexicographic
product result treated in \cite{Vialard2025}. For finite $\eta$, the same
expression simply says $\eta n$. Its appearance here is a consistency
check and a consequence of the more general finite-block argument, not
an assertion that the uniform problem remained open.

\subsection{A small tail example}

For two incomparable fibers of types $\omega+1$ and $\omega+2$, no term
is truncated, giving $\omega\cdot2+3$. If the first fiber is below the
second, its constant term is deleted, giving $\omega\cdot2+2$.
This is why ordinary concatenation of whole impure fibers is not a
sufficient lower-bound construction for arbitrary partial index orders:
maximal linearizations may separate their Cantor-normal-form blocks.
The pure-stratum theorem avoids that difficulty by starting at the
appropriate block granularity.

\section{A compact proof-dependency audit}\label{sec:audit}

The proof graph has three standard inputs: maximal-linearization
attainment, the disjoint-union/natural-sum formula, and the
Cartesian-product/natural-product formula. Induced-subposet monotonicity
and elementary Cantor-normal-form arithmetic are used throughout.

From these, Lemma~\ref{lem:absorption} proves the crucial upper bound for a
cofinal pure component. Theorem~\ref{thm:strata} adds finite grouping and
an explicit block-order lower bound. Lemmas~\ref{lem:frontiers},
\ref{lem:canonical} and \ref{lem:cofinalfrontier} establish the finite
frontier model without any ordinal estimate. The product sandwich then
feeds Theorem~\ref{thm:strata}, yielding Theorem~\ref{thm:general}.

The height proof is separate. Its upper bound is an explicit ordinal
rank map, and its lower bound is an explicit chain. It does not infer
height from maximal order type or from a finite numerical test.
The counting and pure-output results are finite corollaries of the
frontier formula. Finally, Appendix~\ref{sec:lex} uses maximal
linearizations only after proving that fiber replacement preserves the
quantity being calculated.

No step depends on a claim that cofinality preserves arbitrary maximal
order types, that frontier strata are fully comparable, that finite
cutoffs compute infinite ranks, or that all ordinal widths equal maximal
order types. The counterexamples and limitations in
Section~\ref{sec:boundaries} are included to make these potential failure
modes explicit.
